# LangzeitPlaner v2 — UX AUDIT AGAINST THE 196-ITEM CATALOGUE

| | |
|---|---|
| **Audited** | `ab00471` on `main`, 2026-09-11/12 |
| **Build under audit** | **v2.0.0-rc.3** — the Tauri shell (`src-tauri/`), which is what the DMG ships. The Swift shell (`shell-macos/main.swift`) is the reference the tier-2 suite runs in, and is audited only where the two disagree. |
| **Catalogue** | `docs/v2/UX-AUDIT-CATALOGUE.md` v1.0 (2026-08-25) — 196 auditable items: 130 canonical stories, 46 derived behaviours, 20 committed absences. The 10 O-series items are excluded by the catalogue's own instruction. |
| **Method** | One auditor per section (23) produced a verdict and evidence per item · one skeptic per section opened every cited PASS and tried to overturn it · two independent defenders attacked every FAIL/PARTIAL/ABSENT-VIOLATED from the product's side · this report reconciles the merged result. 136 agents, ~1 750 tool calls. |
| **Precedence** | On conflict the catalogue governs this report; the catalogue says Spec v1.0 and Addendum v2.1 govern it. Items marked *(am.)* were audited in their amended wording. |
| **Evidence rule** | Every verdict cites something a person can open: `file:line`, a test file and row title, or a command and its decisive output. A document alone is `doc-read` and capped at low confidence. |

---

## The answer

**150 of 196 items hold** (134 PASS, 16 committed absences confirmed). **4 fail outright, 36 hold in part, 3 committed absences are violated.** One item is not verifiable from this machine, one `[C]` is a noted gap, and 13.4 is superseded by 21.5 exactly as the catalogue says.

**One cluster blocks shipping to a non-technical family member, and it is not in the family features at all — it is the Ablage menu.** In the shipped Tauri build, *all three* of Exportieren (⇧⌘E), Importieren (⇧⌘I) and Drucken (⌘P) are broken: the two dialogs deadlock the app on its main thread, and print has no native command behind it at all. Every one of them works in the Swift reference shell, which is why 5 876 green rows never saw it — tier 2 runs the Swift shell. This is the audit's headline; it sits in F11/F12, not in F15–F22. It also invalidates the backup instruction the product itself gives during family onboarding (21.B) and the ⌘⇧E step at the top of the install-over-v1 rollback ladder.

**The Familienkreis itself is in good shape.** F15–F20 carry no FAIL; their PARTIALs are named missing edges (no UI to set a category's default visibility, no leave-time tombstone, a join screen that does not pre-empt a taken colour) rather than broken promises. The privacy core holds: 20.5, 21.1 and 21.2 pass with tests that would go red if they regressed, and no role can reach another member's Privat content.

**Three committed absences are violated, and two of them are known and priced.** N10/21.5 — solo mode is no longer literally zero-request, because the updater fetches a manifest once the welcome card is dismissed; that is the PO's own D10 ruling and STATUS.md already rewords the promise, but the catalogue's wording is what this audit measures, so it is recorded as violated. N16/1.B — the board does scroll vertically below a window height the shipped `minHeight` permits. N4 — times of day appear in three places (snapshot list, sync status, updater status), none of them on an entry.

**One item is contested** (19.5, whether the passphrase on the encrypted backup counts as "a password in this product"): one defender refuted the finding, one did not. That is a PO ruling, not an engineering question.

## Defects

43 items — 41 `[M]`, 2 `[C]` — ranked by what a user actually hits. Each survived two independent defenders whose job was to show the product passes; `status` records whether both agreed the finding stands.

### ⛔ The Ablage menu — the shipping blocker

**Exportieren, Importieren and Drucken are all broken in the shipped Tauri build, and all three work in the Swift reference shell.** That divergence is exactly why 5 876 green rows never saw it: tier 2 runs the Swift shell.

The two dialogs share one root cause. `export_board` (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1306`) and `import_board` (`:1326`) are **synchronous** `#[tauri::command]` functions that call `blocking_save_file()` / `blocking_pick_file()`. The plugin's own docstring forbids precisely this:

> `tauri-plugin-dialog-2.7.3/src/lib.rs:756-760` — *"This is a blocking operation, and should **NOT** be used when running on the main thread. See `Self::save_file` for a non-blocking version for use in main-thread contexts."* — and the example immediately below it uses `async fn my_command`.

The chain that puts it on the main thread, verified link by link in the exact crate versions in `src-tauri/Cargo.lock`:

1. `wry-0.55.1/src/wkwebview/class/url_scheme_handler.rs:57` — `start_task` is the Obj-C `webView:startURLSchemeTask:`, which WebKit calls **on the main thread**, and it calls the Tauri handler inline.
2. `tauri-2.11.5/src/ipc/protocol.rs:75` → `webview.on_message(...)` → `src/webview/mod.rs:1909` `manager.run_invoke_handler(invoke)` — no `spawn` anywhere on that path.
3. `tauri-macros-2.6.3/src/command/wrapper.rs:251,266` — a **sync** command is `ExecutionContext::Blocking`, so `body_blocking` runs the function body inline.
4. `tauri-plugin-dialog/src/lib.rs:76-85` `blocking_fn!` — `sync_channel(0)`, call `save_file(cb)`, then **`rx.recv()`**.
5. `.../src/desktop.rs:202-211` `save_file` → `run_on_main_thread(...)`, and `tauri-runtime-wry-2.11.4/src/lib.rs:239` runs it **inline** when already on the main thread → `rfd-0.16.0/.../modal_future.rs:78-102`, `app.isRunning() && win.is_some()` → `beginSheetModalForWindow:completionHandler:`, which returns immediately and needs the run loop to deliver its completion.
6. The main thread is now parked in `rx.recv()`. The run loop never turns, the completion never fires, `tx.send` never happens. **The app hangs.**

Print is a plainer absence: `print.js:62-70` calls `invoke('print_board')` whenever the bridge exists and returns, so `window.print()` at `:73` is unreachable — and **`print_board` does not exist in the Tauri shell** (`grep -c print_board src-tauri/src/lib.rs` → 0; `generate_handler![]` at `lib.rs:1487-1533` does not list it). `shell-macos/main.swift:1829` has it, with a full `NSPrintInfo` landscape/fit operation at `:2468-2488`. ⌘P therefore logs a warning and does nothing.

> **Note on provenance.** The auditors found 11.7 (export) and 12.4 (print). The identical deadlock in `import_board` was found during this reconciliation, not by the agent pass, and is recorded under 11.3 below with its own evidence. It is the same defect one function down.

> **This has already cost something.** The install-over-v1 instruction in `docs/v2/SHIP.md` and the rollback ladder both begin *"⌘⇧E export"*. On rc.3 that step hangs. Anyone following it should take the file copy of `board.json` / `snapshots.json` instead, which does not go through the app.

A non-async command is generated as `body_block
- *Defender 2* — could not refute, suggests **FAIL**: The finding stands for the shipped (Tauri) shell, and it is sharper than "inferred": every link of the chain is in source at HEAD ab00471 and pinned crate versions. src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1306-1322 declares `export_board` as a plain sync `#[tauri::command]` (no `async`, no `(async)`), and calls `.blocking_save_file()` (:1313). #### 12.4 `[M]` — FAIL · confirmed · F12

> Printing goes through the macOS print dialog — "Als PDF sichern", paper size, and scale-to-fit come free and native.

**Failing clause:** *"Printing goes through the macOS print dialog — "Als PDF sichern", paper size, and scale-to-fit come free and native."*

**Evidence.** src/js/print.js:62-70 — `const invoke = window.__TAURI__?.core?.invoke; if (invoke) { invoke('print_board', {}).catch((e) => console.warn('[print] bridge failed', e)); return; }` — `window.print()` at :73 is unreachable inside the shell. src-tauri/tauri.conf.json:26 `"withGlobalTauri": true` (so `window.__TAURI__.core.invoke` exists in the DMG). src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1487-1533 — the full generate_handler![] list (load_board … sync_status) has no `print_board`; lib.rs contains no `.print(`, no `NSPrint`, no print command (grep). lib.rs:1423 registers the menu item 'print' / CmdOrCtrl+P which :1553-1563 forwards as emit('menu') → src/js/main.js:559 `print: () => printBoard()` → same dead end. Contrast shell-macos/main.swift:1829 (`case "print_board": printAction?()`) and :2468-2488 (NSPrintInfo landscape, `.fit` pagination, `printOperation(with:)`, `showsPrintPanel = true`, `runModal`) — the reference shell only. .github/workflows/release.yml builds the DMG from src-tauri (Tauri).

- *Defender 1* — could not refute, suggests **FAIL**: The finding stands and is, if anything, understated. In the shipped Tauri build every entry point to printing (toolbar button, ⌘P keydown, and the native "Drucken …" menu item) ends in `invoke('print_board', {})`, and the Tauri shell registers no such command, so the promise rejects and the rejection is swallowed by `.catch(console.warn)`. No macOS print dialog can appear; "Als PDF sichern", paper size and scale-to-fit are therefore not merely non-native — they are absent. The `window.print()` fallback at print.js:72 is unreachable inside the DMG because `withGlobalTauri: true` guarantees `win
- *Defender 2* — could not refute, suggests **FAIL**: The auditor read the right shell. In the shipped Tauri build every print entry point (toolbar button, ⌘P keydown, native Ablage → Drucken … menu item) funnels into printBoard(), which — because window.__TAURI__.core.invoke exists under withGlobalTauri:true — calls invoke('print_board') and returns before ever reaching window.print(). lib.rs registers no print_board command (the generate_handler! list ends at sync_status; the only 'print' hits in lib.rs are comments and the menu-item label), Cargo.toml pulls no printer plugin, capabilities/default.json grants no core:webview:allow-print, and no

- *Defender 2* — could not refute, suggests **FAIL**: The finding stands. I checked every screen in the flow (renderCreateForm, renderCreated, renderJoinForm, renderJoined) and every German string they draw (i18n.js:350-458): the closest thing to the catalogue sentence is circleJoinLead 'Kein Konto, kein Passwort, keine Anmeldung' (i18n.js:399-400), shown only on the join form, with no backup clause; the create side has not even that. closeCircleScree

The plugin forbids it in as many words (tauri-plugin-dialog-2.7.3/src/lib.rs:756-760). The `<input type=file>` fallback at backup.js:53-62 never runs, because a deadlocked call never throws. Swift shell is unaffected (main.swift uses NSOpenPanel directly). ‖ AGENT-PASS EVIDENCE (for the milder clause): Import door: src/js/backup.js:42-84 (⇧⌘I main.js:522; Settings settings.js:390-391) → confirmSheet title i18n `importConfirm` 'Aktuelles Board ersetzen?' / 'Replace current board?' (src/js/i18n.js:57, :543), confirm label 'Ersetzen', danger:true → store.replaceAll(parsed) (src/js/store.js:5527). Restore of a v1-shape export pinned by tests/tier1/store-persistence.test.js describe 'replaceAll() (11.3)' (:812-866, incl. #### 11.2 `[M]` — PARTIAL · confirmed · F11

> One click exports my entire own state — private entries, my shared/Belegt entries with their flags, categories, settings, and my identity keys — as a single JSON; the file is the recovery artifact and says so in its header; other members' entries are never included. *(A2)*

**Failing clause:** *"One click exports my entire own state — … my shared/Belegt entries with their flags, … and my identity keys — as a single JSON; the file is the recovery artifact and says so in its header"*

**Evidence.** The one-click export (⇧⌘E src/js/main.js:521; Settings › Daten › Exportieren src/js/settings.js:388-389; menu action main.js:557) is src/js/backup.js:15-40 → store.exportJSON() (src/js/store.js:6328-6330) → exportV1JSON (src/js/core/materialize.js:1119) over stripV2Fields (:1086-1105), whose whitelist V1_ENTRY_FIELDS (:1035-1039) is ['id','date','text','categoryId','repeatsYearly'] / ['id','startDate','endDate','label','categoryId'] — no `visibility`, no `coEdit`, no identity block, no header field. Foreign entries are dropped (:1097-1098 `!n.isForeign`), so 'other members' entries are never included' holds. The genuine recovery artifact (src/js/crypto/backup.js exportBackup :1131, _README_de/_README_en header, ownBoardOnly :723, BACKUP_ENTRY_FIELDS :269-270 adds visibility+coEdit, sealed identity) is reachable only from src/js/family/familysettings.js:1097-1100 buildRecoverySection, which returns without drawing unless a family space exists AND hooks.recoveryMaterial is armed — a solo Mac never sees it — and it is two buttons plus a passphrase field (:1140-1148), saved as `langzeitplaner-schluessel-<date>.json` (:1211-1213). Tests: tests/tier1/store-persistence.test.js 'exportJSON is the entire state — entries, categories AND settings' (:1194) green; tests/tier1/crypto-backup.test.js 'A2: every device-identifying decoration is stripped, and the flags survive' (:325) green (78/78) — but that pins the library, not the one-click door.

- *Defender 1* — could not refute, suggests **PARTIAL**: The auditor's chain is correct at HEAD ab00471 and I could not find a path that refutes it. The one-click export (⇧⌘E, Settings › Daten, menu 'export') produces the v1-shape JSON via stripV2Fields: it contains own private AND own shared/Belegt entries (only `isForeign` is filtered), categories, scratchpads and settings, and excludes other members' entries — those clauses hold. But the whitelist V1_ENTRY_FIELDS drops `visibility` and `coEdit` (both are real note/bar truth registers in ops.js), the file carries no identity block and no header beyond `schemaVersion: 1`, and the tier-1 row 'export
- *Defender 2* — could not refute, suggests **PARTIAL**: The finding stands and is, if anything, understated. The one-click export (⇧⌘E, File › Exportieren …, Settings › Daten › Exportieren) is exportBoard() → store.exportJSON() → exportV1JSON → stripV2Fields, a whitelist that keeps only v1 fields; visibility and coEdit are dropped, there is no identity block and no header. I ran exportV1JSON over a state with a 'geteilt'/coEdit note and a 'belegt' bar and the output carries neither flag ("has visibility: false \| has coEdit: false \| has identity: false \| has _README: false"). Tier-2 pins (ran at HEAD per the brief; assertions opened and confirmed): dom-rendering.dom.js rows 3.1/3.2, 3.2 (three months), 3.7, 3.6 ×3, 3.8; interaction.dom.js rows 'a vertical drag…', 'dragging upward normalises…', 'the +n chip opens the same popover'; board-render.dom.js rows 'a bar renders one stripe plus one label…', 'a bar past the horizon…', 'a fourth overlapping bar…'; coedit.dom.js §4b/§4d. (2) Lane stability (3.A) has one designed exception after commit: the per-column lane rescue (DESIGN-DECISIONS §C, layout.test.js 'RESCUE — a rescued bar may sit in DIFFERENT lanes in different months', and its winner is store-order dependent, flagged there for LZP-402). (3) Tests I would want that do not exist: a tier-2 row asserting `.bar-preview` exists and tracks the pointer mid-create-drag (3.B), and a row asserting every other `.bar`'s `dataset.lane` and `style.right` are byte-identical across a 200-move drag (3.A); today both are code-read only. Minor inaccuracies not affecting verdicts: 3.8's cited '+n chip opens the same popover' row seeds 5 notes, not bars (the catalogue clause only requires marker + tooltip, which the 3.8 dom-rendering row asserts directly); 3.A's note says the move/resize preview sits 'not in a lane' but `right: 2px` is lane 0's offset. No shell-level behaviour in this section, so the Tauri/Swift distinction does not apply.

Tier-1 suites run at HEAD for this section, all green: palette (6/6), layout (93/93), store-persistence (125/125), core-undo (97/97), core-ops (70/70), property/project-domains (13/13), attack/redaction-invariants (49/49). Tier-2 rows cited were read and their assertions match the clauses; note that tests/COVERAGE.md's F4 table is stale in one cell — 4.4's 'sheet dropdown flow not driven' is now driven in retrofit-probe.dom.js P5 and retrofit-probe7.dom.js W1. Tests I would want that do not exist: (a) a tier-2 row that asserts the '.flash' class (and its 600 ms removal) on the legend item after creating into a hidden category — P8 only logs it; (b) a tier-2 row that clicks a swatch in the category manager's `.swatches` and asserts the board/legend recolour (recolor is only pinned at op level); (c) a distinguishability check for 4.5 (pairwise contrast/ΔE across the 10 hexes) — today only distinctness of hex strings is pinned. Residuals from tests/COVERAGE.md touching this section: item 4 (DE rename drops nameEn — wording defect) and item 5 (paletteRef repair degenerates to 'blau'). Cross-cutting: 4.A's wording conflicts with 19.4 ('my entire board syncs between my devices') — categories are personal-space ops that replicate to the user's own Macs while never entering the family space; the catalogue should carry an erratum so 'never synced' reads 'never shared with the family', and should say whether 'Meine Kategorien'/'Familie' are required labels (the shipped legend deliberately shows neither, to avoid the 'Familie' category/section collision in one 40 px row). Nothing in F4 is shell-level; the Tauri vs Swift shell distinction does not apply to this section.

**Skeptic note.** All six F4 verdicts survive at HEAD ab00471. Every cited file:line exists and says what the auditor claims; no cited test is skipped/todo/only; the five tier-1 suites reproduce the cited counts (store-persistence 125/125, core-undo 97/97, core-ops 70/70, layout 93/93, palette 6/6, all via `node --test --import ./tests/helpers/dev-flag.mjs tests/tier1/<file>`). F4 is pure webview behaviour, so the Tauri-vs-Swift shell question does not arise. Two wording imprecisions in the evidence that do not change any verdict: (1) 4.1 cites retrofit-probe.dom.js P10 for rename, but P10 asserts only `depth()-before === 1`; the actual rename-through-the-sheet assertion lives in retrofit-probe3.dom.js R2 (`dc.name === 'Zuhause'`, undo restores 'Familie'/'Family'), which the auditor also cites. (2) 4.2 describes `categoryId: { t:'id' }` at ops.js:84/:95 as "required"; ops.js:386 only validates the value when present (non-empty string). The clause still holds because every create builder (ops.js:842-848, :886-892, :944-950) and every UI site (interact.js:542, :731→:799, popover.js:270) supplies it, migrate() repairs orphans (store.js:435-439), and store.category() falls back to the first category (store-persistence 1428-1432). Independently confirmed for 4.6 that the flash cannot be wiped by a late redraw: store.subscribe→redraw→renderLegend is synchronous inside apply() (main.js:88-93, :277-280), and the ResizeObserver path (legend.js:406-411) repaints only on a ≥0.5 px width change, so `flashCategory` after apply() lands on the freshly built node. Confirmed no tier-2 row clicks a `.swatches .sw` in the category sheet (grep over tests/tier2), matching the auditor's disclosure; recolor rests on legend.js:534-543 code-read plus core-undo 825/1054 at tier 1, which is thin but honest for the stated 'high' given the handler is a one-line apply().

**Skeptic note.** All five F10 verdicts survive at HEAD ab00471. What I checked and what I would add to the record: 10.1 — board-render.dom.js:101 asserts 12 `.pad textarea` with data-month deepEqual to model().cols keys; dom-rendering.dom.js:1316 asserts 12 pads on an empty board; board.js:1260-1273 renderPad appends a real `<textarea rows=5>` after head and rows (board.js:399 'head · rows · pad', :616-617); layout.js:852 keys the pad by monthKey(y,m) (dates.js:56 = `YYYY-MM`). Every clause holds. 10.2 — interaction.dom.js:323 exercises input → real 600 ms debounce → store → force-redraw keeps value; e13-glass §G4 (:485) and §G4b (:518) assert same node, same value, caret kept, blur commits the whole sentence. Disk persistence is the shared path: store.apply (:4562) → _commit → schedulePersist (:4509) → persistNow serializes `this.state` (:5667-5668), and store-persistence.test.js:116/:1205 pin that the document/dump carries `scratchpads`. Confirmed AUDIT.md:1192 still says FAIL/F7 and STATUS.md:166 records the closure — the AUDIT table row is stale, as the auditor said. Ran store-persistence: 125/125. 10.4 — entities.js:150-152 rejects any pad id that is not `YYYY-MM` (core-ops :767 throws on '2026-9'); layout.js:852 `\|\| ''` renders a keyless month empty; the only key deletion is the pad emptying (ops.js:1414 / materialize.js:963-966); oplog.compact (:984-991) folds registers to a checkpoint rather than dropping them, so age never removes a pad. core-ops :1385-1389 pins multi-year coexistence. Ran core-ops 70/70, core-materialize 90/90. The auditor's caveat (no glass row pages between Aug '26 and Aug '27) is accurate but the structural pin is sufficient for the wording. 10.A — the one open half was "its own text undo". Both shells displace the native `undo:` responder with a custom accelerator (Swift main.swift:2549 item(...,"undo","z") → emitMenu :2460-2463 → __lzpEmit; Tauri lib.rs:1435 MenuItem::with_id "undo" CmdOrCtrl+Z → :1562 app.emit("menu", id)) and both land in the same main.js:549-553 menuUndo → document.execCommand('undo') when a TEXTAREA is focused; retrofit-probe4 S3 (:197) pins only the negative half (board stack untouched). Nothing in the repo asserts execCommand('undo') actually reverts textarea text in WebKit, so I probed it in a throwaway WKWebView (nonPersistent data store, no Keychain, no shared data dir; scratchpad/undoprobe.swift): result `{"ins":true,"before":"Hallo Welt","sup":true,"en":true,"ok":true,"after":""}` — WebKit supports and executes DOM-sourced undo on a textarea. The positive half therefore holds in the engine PRODUCTION ships; medium remains right only because "reads as margin" is a stylesheet inference (app.css:786-799, print.css:123). 10.B — entities.js:73 FAMILY_OF has no pad; core-ops :314 `pad.set` is personal-space; e7-leak-routes :462-468 asserts no family-space kind may address 'pad'; the outbox filter is the CODE at store.js:2441 `if (op.space !== sp) continue;` (the cited :2409-2412 is the docstring above it — correct the citation, the evidence is real); privacy-e5-scope §5 (:299) shows a padBlur converging onto the same member's other Mac via the personal space, which the wording permits. Ran e7-leak-routes 49/49, privacy-e5-scope 12/12. Every cited test row exists, is not skipped/todo, and the tier-1/attack suites were re-run here; the tier-2 rows cited are none of the three residual-FAIL rows (§A4/§E1/§E3).

**Section note.** Where the section lives: shortcuts in src/js/main.js:492-579 (keymap + 'menu' event bridge); the native half in src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1343-1571 (set_shell_pref, build_menu, tray, window events). The shipped DMG is the Tauri build (.github/workflows/release.yml:290 'cargo tauri build'), so native verdicts are about lib.rs — and every tier-2 shell row ran in the Swift shell, which is why the three shell divergences found here are invisible to the suite: (1) no window-frame persistence in Tauri (13.1); (2) no RunEvent::Reopen handler, so a Dock click after ⌘W does nothing — tao returns NO from applicationShouldHandleReopen when no window is visible (13.5); (3) the Tauri menu bar is hard-coded German and set_shell_pref's bool-typed `value` rejects the 'language' string the JS sends (13.7). All three are implemented in shell-macos/main.swift (2058-2060, 2441-2445, 2450-2453). A fourth, shell-independent conflict: holiday and Ferien names are translated in EN (layout.js:564/567 → holidays.js:111, ferien.js:254) while 13.7 says they remain German, and tests/tier1/dates-holidays.test.js:740 pins the translated names — the catalogue and that test disagree and need an erratum one way or the other. Neither shell builds a Hilfe menu (13.K). Prior-audit residuals touched: docs/v2/AUDIT.md:1198 marked 13.1/13.2/13.3/13.5 UNVERIFIABLE and :1199 marked 13.6/13.7 PASS with a 'no test enumerates the map' gap — this pass resolves the first four by code-read on the shipped shell (two of them to PARTIAL) and downgrades 13.7 to PARTIAL. Tests that do not exist and would pin regressions here: a keymap enumeration that dispatches each ⌘-combination against the page and asserts the effect (only ⌘Z is dispatched today, unasserted, retrofit-probe2.dom.js:438; no Backspace/Delete keydown anywhere in tier 2); an Escape-against-open-popover row; and a cargo-side check that set_shell_pref accepts the 'language' pref (or that main.js stops sending it). Tier-1 runs this session: repeats-find-i18n.test.js 121/121, headless-shell.test.js 29/29, dmg-contents.test.js 19/19 — all pass at HEAD ab00471. Nothing under $HOME/Library/Application Support/LangzeitPlaner was touched and no forbidden script was run.

**Skeptic note.** All cited evidence exists at HEAD ab00471 and was opened. Keymap main.js:492-532 and the Tauri menu accelerators lib.rs:1397-1457 match the catalogue key-for-key (⌘Z/⇧⌘Z, ⌘F, ⌘T, ⌘P, ⌘, , ⌘1/⌘2, ⇧⌘E/⇧⌘I, ⌘W via 'hide-window' → w.hide() at lib.rs:1555-1560, ⌘Q via muda PredefinedMenuItem::quit — predefined.rs:339 confirms CmdOrCtrl+Q). set_shell_pref/export_board/import_board are in generate_handler! (lib.rs:1497-1499). Tier-2 rows cited for 13.A/B/E/I/J/L were opened and assert what the items claim (interaction.dom.js:167-199, 73, 96, 222, 695, 733; shell-bridge.dom.js:103; datenschutz.dom.js:71-93 opens the REAL sheet via openSettings()). Two corrections that do not change verdicts: (a) 13.J's note says no tier-2 row dispatches Backspace — wrong; tests/tier2/retrofit-probe.dom.js:163 'P1 delete via Backspace' dispatches key(document.body,'Backspace') on a selected note and asserts notes.length===0 and one undo step, so 13.J is stronger than stated (test-read for the binding itself). (b) 13.L's family-settings.dom.js rows run buildFamilySections into a DETACHED body (family-settings.dom.js:46-51), not the real sheet; the real-sheet wiring is code-read (mount.js:187-195 setFamilySections → settings.js:380-382), and in solo mode the members/admin sections return early (membersui.js:795, adminpanel.js guards) — consistent with 'no circle', so high stands. 13.3 stays medium: the autostart path to auto-launch's ~/Library/LaunchAgents/{app}.plist (auto-launch-0.5.0/src/macos.rs:63-64) is complete but never exercised. Ambiguities not counted against 13.A: whether WKWebView hands ⌘Z to the page's keydown or to the native menu first, each path lands on store.undo exactly once (main.js:505-514 vs 549-556), so no double-fire either way.

**Section note.** Where F15 lives: src/js/family/createjoin.js (create/join screens, settings entry section, invite code derivation, paste parsing), src/js/family/membersui.js (member list, self-edit, colour rule, legend chips), src/js/family/adminpanel.js (invite list/expiry/revoke), src/js/family/mount.js (the ports and the solo/circle mount gates), src/js/main.js:120-227 (the single dynamic import() door), server/core/handlers/invites.js (TTL, single-use, revoke). Nothing in F15 is shell-level: neither shell-macos/main.swift nor src-tauri/src/lib.rs has a Familienkreis code path; the Tauri shell only matters here through the updater (15.1 caveat) and the sync_request transport. Tests run at HEAD ab00471 in this session: tests/fleet/e10-fleet-circle.test.js (14/14 pass, ~1 s), tests/tier1/createjoin.test.js + tests/tier1/network-scope.test.js (52/52 pass). Tier-2 rows cited were opened and their assertions match the clauses; none of the three residual tier-2 FAIL rows (§A4/§E1/§E3) touch F15. Cross-cutting: (1) 15.1's 'no network activity' is the same fact as 21.5/N10 vs 22.3 — the catalogue contradicts itself, and the prior audit (docs/v2/AUDIT.md:149, :1208) split it as 15.1 PASS / 21.5 FAIL; I have marked 15.1 PARTIAL on the literal clause so the PO sees the contradiction in this section too. The update check is consent-gated by a sentence on the v1 welcome card (main.js:624-645) whose dismissal counts as disclosure — worth a PO look at whether that is the 'told once' 22.3 intends. (2) Server authorisation for mint/revoke is any active member (requireActiveMember), tighter only in the UI — a note for F20 (admin verbs), not a defect in 15.5. (3) The AUDIT.md 'F15 · LOW' block (lines 1145-1170) is about the security audit's F15 finding (withoutForeignEntries), not this catalogue section; item 1 is closed by tests/tier1/spine-foreign-guard.test.js, items 2-7 are open docs/blast-radius notes. (4) Tests I would want that do not exist: a tier-2 row asserting the 'Einladung kopieren' button on the create-done screen writes invitationText to the clipboard (15.2's 'shareable' is code-read only); and a row pinning that the join screen's pre-redemption picker offers every tone (so a future 'taken colours in the invitation' change is visible).

**Skeptic note.** All five F15 verdicts (15.2, 15.3, 15.4, 15.5, 15.6) upheld at the stated confidence. Verified at HEAD ab00471: (a) every cited tier-2 row exists, is live (no skip/todo), and its assertions cover the catalogue clause as worded — family-createjoin.dom.js:294-343 (one screen: #circle-name, #circle-display, PALETTE swatches; code matches Crockford pattern; role === admin; relay saw exactly POST /spaces then POST /invites), :396-443 (zero password/email inputs, paste normalises and takes origin, redeem body keys exactly [colorRef, device, inviteId, member, proof], role member); family-members.dom.js:215-233, :254-274, :276-304; family-admin.dom.js:245-256, :306-318, :634-644; join-paste.dom.js:411+ control row. (b) I ran tests/fleet/e10-fleet-circle.test.js myself: 14 pass / 0 fail / 0 skipped, including §15.2, §15.3, §15.4, §15.5a/b/c, §15.6; and tests/tier1/createjoin.test.js: 31 pass. (c) The fleet §15.6 helper (e10:120-122) calls the real store.apply('setMyProfile'), the same op the shipped port wires at src/js/family/mount.js:282-289 (core/ops.js:1149 registers it), so the tier-2 fake-port rows and the fleet propagation rows close the chain end to end. (d) Code paths confirmed: createjoin.js:1807-1836 done step renders #circle-code, TTL hint (INVITE_UI.ttlDays = 7 at :127, matching server INVITE_TTL_MS 604800000 at invites.js:52 and used at :192) and 'Einladung kopieren' via ports.clipboard(invitationText) with a fallback toast that the code is on screen — so 'shareable' does not depend on clipboard success; createjoin.js:1855-1955 renderJoinForm is one screen (paste, optional origin row from originField() at :1652, name, colour picker, submit); membersui.js:792 gates the member section only on a circle existing, not on role; chipNode (membersui.js) paints colorRef and initial; familysettings.js:175-177 mounts members section for everyone; mount.js is dynamically imported from main.js:133; invites.js:307 usedAt pre-check, :318 atomic consumeInvite, :308-309 revoked/expired → invite_invalid, :420-437 revokeInvite; adminpanel.js:869 buildInvites only for isAdmin, :1098 expiryLabel, :1104-1111 revoke button. (e) 15.6 'anytime': selfEditSupported (membersui.js:429) requires only a setProfile port, which the shipped mount always supplies when a circle exists — not gated on keysPending or sync state. All of F15's behaviour is web-layer, so no Swift/Tauri shell divergence applies. **Skeptic note.** All six verdicts survive at HEAD ab00471. I re-ran every citable pure-node suite (tests/fleet/e10-fleet-board 12/0, e9-attack-coedit 11/0, e9-attack-restore 7/0, e9-attack-moderation 10/0, e9-attack-diverge 8/0, e12-unshare 8/0, tests/tier1/conflict 31/0, tests/tier1/unshare 26/0) and opened every cited tier-2 assertion and code path. Nothing skipped/todo. Three citation corrections the parent should carry into the ledger, none of which change a verdict or confidence: (1) 18.1 — coedit.dom.js §2d (:350-366) builds `foreignNote(12, { level:'geteilt', coEdit:true })`, i.e. a CO-EDITABLE foreign entry, not the default-off one the auditor's wording implies. It is therefore a stronger claim for the delete verb (even an invited co-editor gets no delete button), and the default-off case is pinned by §2a (:316-323, no drag), §1c-b (:281-296, applyCoEdit never reached), and popover.js:421-425 which returns the row for ANY `isForeign` entry before the ✕ button at :452 is built. §1d (:298-311) pins ⌫ declining on a co-editable foreign entry. Verdict and high confidence stand. (2) 18.3 — the cited e10-fleet-board row '§18.3 · an admin unshare is non-destructive' (:487-514) does NOT drive an admin unshare: the OWNER (Mama) sets her own note to 'privat' via store.txn, and the test's own comment says so. It should not be cited as admin-unshare evidence. The clause is still established elsewhere on real code: tests/tier1/unshare.test.js §4-a/§4-b (:530-565, real foldFor + materialize on a peer and on the owner), e9-attack-moderation §3a (:116-129) and §3c (:231-249) (admin op minted by the shipped `adminUnshareOp`, peers fold through real applyRemote; only the admin-side seal hooks are stubbed), tier-2 unshare-ui.dom.js 1b/1c/2a/3e/5b (all open and assert what the auditor says), and familysettings.js:827-830 + :994 wiring the shipped default to `createUnshare()`. The one end-to-end row that drives the real `createUnshare().run()` over a real engine is tests/tier2/shell-family-e2e.dom.js:954-982, which is PHASE-gated (skips unless PHASE==='unshare') — I cannot confirm that phase ran in this session's tier-2 pass. Medium confidence is therefore the right ceiling and is what the auditor gave. (3) 18.A — sharing.js `.share-attr` is at :685-686, not :678-679 (which is the downgrade `.share-note`); the cluster is still one `.pop-share` group containing radiogroup (:612-650), co-edit checkbox (:657-668) and attribution in sequence. Trivial line slip. Production wiring confirmed where it matters: the 18.5 notice is installed on the shipped circle path (src/js/main.js:209 → mount.js:384 mountCircleSurfaces → :399 installConflictNotice, `me` resolved from store.diagnostics().identity.memberId at conflict.js:581-583); conflict.dom.js `race()` (:199-204) applies the peer op through the real store.applyRemote. 18.6's "new shared operation" clause is pinned literally by tests/tier1/unshare.test.js 18.6-a (:862-869: new op id, newer stamp, same entity, pub.alive:true) in addition to the fleet row. No shell-level behaviour is involved in F18, so the Tauri/Swift distinction does not bear on these items.

**Section note.** Where F20 lives: src/js/family/adminpanel.js (the section and the relay port), src/js/family/leavedelete.js (LIFECYCLE_COPY, the four confirmations, the co-signature sheets), src/js/family/removal.js (rotation + `member.set{_alive:false}` after a removal), server/core/handlers/lifecycle.js (remove/leave/transfer/rename/delete), with the tier-2 pin in tests/tier2/family-admin.dom.js and the multi-Mac pins in tests/fleet/e6-removal, e6-attack-removed, e6-gate-removal. Everything here is web-layer; the Tauri shell (src-tauri/src/lib.rs) plays no role, so no shell divergence applies. The production relay's 20.4 purge rests on Prisma `onDelete: Cascade` (server/prisma/schema.prisma) — verified present on every Space relation. Two defects found, both with the same shape (a Mac is never told it is out): (1) 20.3 — a voluntary leave authors no in-log op and triggers no rotation, so the leaver's shared entries remain on every remaining board that already folded them; SHELL-VERIFICATION.md F-SHELL-2 (STATUS.md:509, still open) is the same root cause seen from the co-signer count. (2) 20.4 — after a delete (and equally after a 20.2 removal), the non-admin members' Macs stay armed into the vanished circle: sync shows `syncErrAuth`, „Kreis verlassen" fails with 403 not_a_member before `forgetCircle` runs, and 20.6 blocks a new circle. A 403 not_a_member on /members/leave should be treated as 'already out'. Residual ledgers touched: docs/v2/AUDIT.md:1220 marks 20.2 FAIL (F3); STATUS.md:159 says closed — confirmed by running tests/audit/compaction-sweep.test.js (§2b/§2c now assert refusal / no resurrection and pass). That suite's §0a/§0b fail at HEAD (expects 0 retained lines; `_persistOps` deliberately retains admin-chain lines) — stale expectations, not a 20.2 regression, but the suite is red and should be re-titled. tests/attack/ownership-authz-admin.test.js keeps 10 fold-layer rows OPEN (A2*, A3b/A3c, A3d, A4, A5*) — A3b (admin of an invented space evicts a member of yours) and A5 (removed member re-admits herself) touch 20.2 at the authz layer and are bounded only by relay revocation; FINDINGS.md G-1 (WP-9) open. The 'visibility' truth register is never reverted on leave — worth an explicit PO decision (revert on leave vs. keep the person's choice). Tests I would want that do not exist: (a) fleet: member leaves → assert their Geteilt entries leave the remaining boards and an epoch rotation happens; (b) fleet/tier-2: admin deletes → a remaining member's Mac reverts to solo (or at least its leave succeeds); (c) leave, then join a fresh circle → a previously-shared entry is not republished on its next edit; (d) a tier-2 row that the removed/deleted member's phantom circle offers a working exit.

Tier-1/attack/fleet suites run at HEAD in this session, all green: network-scope 21, platform-net 21, platform-updater 33, crypto-guarantees 8, crypto-spacekeys 44, crypto-envelope 53, crypto-backup 78, visibility 28, no-reconstruction 3, sync-personal 75, sync-family 27, headless-shell 29, release-gate 42, dmg-contents 19; attack privacy-e5-* (58), redaction-invariants 49, crypto-relay-read 12, crypto-member-read 13, e7-leak-* (91), e10-network-scope 32, e10-outbound-payload 18, e10-crypto-rules 21; fleet e6-gate-privat 7, e6-attack-privat 16, e6-gate-keys 14. Cross-cutting residuals: (1) The catalogue is internally inconsistent — 21.4/21.5 as written are contradicted by its own 22.3 (daily update check) and 22.8 (releases from GitHub); the PO's D10 amendment (ADR 003 §7.5, 2026-09-03: 'zero UNREQUESTED requests') is not reflected in the catalogue v1.0 (2026-08-25), and even the amended wording names only the feedback press, not the automatic GitHub manifest GET (AUDIT.md:1324 D-F is answered in copy but not in story wording). An erratum on 21.4/21.5 is the honest fix; the code and the Datenschutz copy already say the true thing. (2) In the shipped Tauri shell `UpdaterPrefs.enabled` defaults to true (lib.rs:340) while `SyncPrefs.enabled` defaults to false (lib.rs:808-813); the only consent gate before the first GitHub request is dismissing the welcome card that shows updateCheckHint (main.js:624-645). If the PO wants 'zero' to be literally true for solo installs, flipping that default is the one-line change. (3) All shell-level runtime network rows (tests/tier2/shell-transport, shell-ssrf, network-audit) ran in the Swift reference shell; lib.rs is held to main.swift only by static mirror rows (headless-shell.test.js:734-774, e10-network-scope §1a-§1g) — the rc.3 release proves it compiles, but no runtime SSRF/zero-request measurement has been made against the Tauri binary. (4) Test I would want: a tier-2 row over the create/join screens asserting a backup prompt with the key-loss sentence (21.B) — nothing currently goes red for its absence. (5) tests/tier2/e10-network-scope §5a 'fresh install … ZERO requests' is true only for the pre-disclosure state; a row measuring the post-welcome-card state on a solo install would expose 21.5's literal gap directly rather than through e13-datenschutz §2a's mocked port. (6) Prior ledger: docs/v2/AUDIT.md:1221-1224 (21.1/21.2 PASS, 21.3 FAIL→now closed by F5 rewrite, 21.4 FAIL, 21.5 FAIL) — this audit agrees on 21.1/21.2, closes 21.3, and downgrades 21.4/21.5 to PARTIAL because the sync half and the analytics half demonstrably hold.

**Skeptic note.** All four cited suites re-run at HEAD ab00471 from the repo root with `node --test --import ./tests/helpers/dev-flag.mjs <file>`: crypto-guarantees 8/8, attack/crypto-relay-read 12/12, fleet/e6-gate-privat 7/7, crypto-spacekeys 44/44 — none skipped or todo. 21.1 upheld: the crypto-guarantees row at :167 seals a PERSONAL-space op (sp = mkSpaceId('personal')), so on its own it does not speak to 'Shared and Belegt'; what closes that gap is the production family engine — src/js/sync/family.js:381-409 `sealLine` throws 'Nothing is pushed unencrypted, ever' when no key exists, quarantines on seal failure (:515-519), and the only POST to /api/v1/ops (:527) sends `envelopes` — plus tests/fleet/e6-gate-privat.test.js:206-211 asserting the SHARED entry ('Omas Geburtstag', :37) never appears as text across every byte two real engines received, and :150-152 asserting the raw family stream. Belegt entries travel through the same single seal path (family.js has exactly two transport.request calls, :527 POST and :591 GET). Server schema confirmed at schema.prisma:199-212 (Op.envelope/chain/witness Bytes) and :292-301 (KeyWrap.wrapped Bytes, keyed by recipientId). 21.2 upheld: personal.js:439-449 `assertPersonalSpace` throws on any non-psp_ id and is called at :491 on engine construction; personal.js:765-779 seals with the same 'never unencrypted' refusal; crypto barriers at crypto-guarantees.test.js:249-330 and crypto-spacekeys.test.js:154-173 and :561-575 assert exactly what the auditor says. 21.A upheld: settings.js:439 calls buildDatenschutzSection on every sheet build; :947-1023 renders 25 data-ds blocks; tier-2 §1a (:71) opens the real sheet solo and asserts exactly one 'Datenschutz' section-title with >=12 blocks; scripts/build-frontend.mjs:40 SHIP = ['index.html','src'] copies the whole src tree into the dist/ that src-tauri/tauri.conf.json:7 serves, so the Tauri shell renders the same module. Minor observation, not a downgrade: rows §2a/§2b/§4a build a DETACHED body via section() (datenschutz.dom.js:50-54), not the opened sheet — §1a and §6 (identity of solo paragraph to DATENSCHUTZ.de.soloBody) are what tie that to the real screen. 'Not legalese' has no assertion (§4a bans marketing/survey vocabulary, not legal register); the auditor disclosed this and the copy at :646-800 is conversational du-form German, so high is acceptable on a code-read basis.

**Section note.** Where the section lives: src/js/platform/updater.js (decision logic, 33 tier-1 rows), src/js/update-ui.js (the hint, the settings section, the 22.7 bar), src-tauri/src/lib.rs:182-633 + :1378-1413 + :1475-1535 (the shipped Tauri port: nine update_* commands, App-menu item, plugin init), src-tauri/tauri.conf.json:62-68 (endpoint placeholder + pubkey), .github/workflows/release.yml + .github/scripts/make-update-manifest.mjs (tag → signed DMG + manifest), .github/workflows/server.yml (server guard rails; does not deploy), src/js/firstrun.js (unlock screen), docs/v2/email/* (deliverable 28). Cross-cutting findings: 1. Stale 'UNVERIFIED — never compiled' headers in lib.rs (:186-199, :418-423, :1478) and Cargo.toml (:31, :37, :47): CI's 'Tier 0 — the shell PRODUCTION ships actually compiles' job and the release job both build this crate (run 34643379564 success). The comments now mislead an auditor; docs/v2/AUDIT.md:1225-1227 ('22.1 UNVERIFIABLE', '22.8 UNVERIFIABLE', 'release.yml has never executed') and the AU-14 residual are superseded by three real release runs. 2. Channel state: every release is a prerelease, so `releases/latest/download/latest.json` returns 404 (curl, 2026-09-11). No device has ever received an update through the pipeline; 22.3/22.5 are verified as mechanism only. The first non-prerelease tag is the event that lights the channel — RELEASE-CHECKLIST should name 'watch one rc device converge on it' as an explicit step. 3. `gatekeeper_status` is implemented in neither shell (scripts/build-unlock-page.sh:16; lib.rs invoke_handler). The in-app first-run unlock screen can therefore never auto-show; the DMG read-me and the Settings entry are the only routes. Either implement the command (xattr com.apple.quarantine on Bundle.main) or amend 22.2's wording. 4. Copy drift after the D1 reversal: the mail's step 3, unlockLead/unlockNote and 'Bitte zuerst lesen.html' describe an unsigned wall the notarized build never raises, and the release notes say the app walks the user through the unlock on first start (it cannot). SHIP.md:288-291 records keeping it; worth a PO decision. 5. Literal wording: both 'one plain sentence' strings are two sentences (i18n.js:305 and the 22.7 bar text). Recorded under 22.7, not failed. 6. Tests that do not exist and should: (a) a tier-2/e2e row driving the Tauri shell's update_* commands — every tier-2 updater row runs in the Swift shell, so update_menu_hint's menu rebuild and update_restart's app.restart() have only ever been compiled; (b) a row asserting the endpoint the BUILT binary carries (release-gate §6e checks the pre-flight predicate, and 'The published manifest resolves' is skipped on prereleases); (c) a self-test that verifies the published latest.json signature against tauri.conf.json's pubkey post-publish (today only the key ids were compared here: both abb649a4d8312259). 7. Edge in the shipped plugin (tauri-plugin-updater 2.11.0 updater.rs ~1305-1325): if the bundle path is not user-writable, install falls back to an AppleScript 'with administrator privileges' prompt during the 'quiet' background download — a native password dialog 22.4 does not anticipate. Not reached for a user-dragged copy in /Applications; document or guard. 8. Fleet/multi-device: tests/fleet does not model the updater (it models sync convergence); 22.5's 'converges automatically' is pinned only by the single-channel constants and the one rewritten endpoint.

**Skeptic note.** Verification at HEAD ab00471 (run 34643379564 headSha = ab00471, all steps success; 'Ad-hoc sign' skipped, Apple signing on). Ran: tests/tier1/platform-updater.test.js (# pass 33 / # fail 0), tests/tier1/release-gate.test.js (# pass 42 / # fail 0), .github/scripts/check-email-copy.mjs, scripts/mom-test-probe.mjs (0 FAIL; reads no home dir). Did not run any tier-2, shell or cargo command. Tauri-shell items were checked in src-tauri/src/lib.rs and tauri-plugin-updater-2.11.0/tauri-2.11.5 sources in ~/.cargo, matching src-tauri/Cargo.lock. Cross-cutting facts worth carrying into the residual ledger rather than into any single verdict: (a) `releases/latest/download/latest.json` → HTTP 404 because every published release is a prerelease, so today no installed device can receive anything (release.yml:836 prints this); (b) the JS parser defaults an absent `channel` to 'stable' (updater.js:328), and the published manifest has no channel field — so it parses; (c) on a fresh install the launch check runs before the welcome card is dismissed (main.js:97 vs :111) and stops at 'not-disclosed'; the first real check is the 30-min poll after dismissal — within the 'roughly' of 22.3; (d) the Tauri App-menu hint is wired end to end (lib.rs:1385-1394 → on_menu_event :1553 emits 'menu' → main.js:570 'update-restart' → restartToUpdate) but, as the auditor said, no human or test has ever seen it; (e) lib.rs still carries stale '⚠ UNVERIFIED — never compiled' comments at :414-421 and :1478-1481 although Tier 0 compiles it — comments, not behaviour. No item was found to exhibit contrary behaviour; nothing was overturned.

**Section note.** Where this section really lives: the absences are pinned less by one file than by three sweeps — tests/tier1/network-scope.test.js + tests/attack/e10-network-scope.test.js (N5/N10/N18, exactly one page-side call site, exactly two sanctioned remote hosts), src/js/core/visibility.js + tests/tier1/visibility.test.js + tests/attack/e7-leak-*.test.js (N8/N12/N19/N20), and the FORBIDDEN_CLAIMS sweep in src/js/family/sharing.js:178 exercised by tier-1 unshare.test.js §6-c and tier-2 belegt-render §6 / unshare-ui 4b / unshare-owner §6 (N8/N17). All tier-1/attack files cited were run at HEAD ab00471: network trio 82/82, repeats+palette 127/127, visibility/unshare/authz/leak/silence 354/354. Four catalogue errata are needed, because the catalogue (2026-08-25) predates PO rulings or contradicts the ADRs: (1) N10/21.5 — the PO reworded 21.5 on 2026-09-03 to 'zero UNREQUESTED network requests' (docs/v2/STATUS.md:366-374, AUDIT.md:619-650, LZP-1009 solo feedback POST at lib.rs:1012-1019) and 22.3's opt-in update check reaches a second host (github.com); N10 is violated only in wording. (2) N1/19.5/21.B — ADR 002 §7.2 designs an optional backup passphrase ('Backup mit Passwort sichern', familysettings.js:1117-1125); 'no passwords' is true of accounts, false of the export sheet. (3) N4 — three settings lines show hh:mm timestamps (settings.js:412, syncstatus.js:645/690, update-ui.js:524/544); 11.A says 'list of 7 dates'. (4) N16/1.B — .board-wrap is overflow-y:auto with fixed row heights (app.css:209-212), the shipped minHeight is 640 px (tauri.conf.json) against ~770 px needed at 22 px rows, and the product's own copy (settings.js:288-290) and tier-2 density-808 §8 treat vertical scroll as a warned trade, not an impossibility. Residuals in prior ledgers this section touches: docs/v2/AUDIT.md D10/21.5 (CLOSED by ruling), privacy-e5-silence §4 'P-2' (documentation of the release host — now asserted present in server-metadata.md), e7-leak-downgrade FINDING E7L-2 (→Privat on a cursor-behind Mac emits no retraction; a 16.5 gap, adjacent to N8), crypto-relay-read length side-channel ≥140 bytes (16.7, adjacent to N12). Shipped-shell specifics: the Tauri shell (lib.rs) has no notification plugin, no dock badge API and no native dialog beyond file open/save (lib.rs:1305-1330); update_menu_hint (439-450) only rebuilds the App menu. Tests I would want that do not exist: a tier-2 row asserting the board fits without vertical overflow at the shipped minimum window size (or a CSS rule making .board-wrap overflow-y:hidden with density auto-fit); a tier-2 row scanning board+toolbar text for hh:mm patterns; a tier-2 button scan (like sync-status.dom.js:104-117) that runs with the family settings sheet open, so N2 is asserted over the whole settings surface rather than prose rows.

## Verification of this report

The agent pass was reconciled and then checked mechanically, because an audit that cites evidence nobody opened is worth no more than the opinions it replaced.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Every one of the 196 catalogue ids appears in the ledger exactly once, with a verdict from the fixed vocabulary | **pass** — 196 rows, 0 missing, 0 duplicated, 0 extra |
| The ledger's verdicts sum to the counts in the header table | **pass** — recomputed from the tables, not copied |
| Every repo path cited by a PASS / ABSENT-CONFIRMED row exists, and every cited line number is inside its file | **pass** — 689 refs across 157 files; 0 missing, 0 past EOF |
| Every cited test-row title exists somewhere in `tests/` | **pass** — 217 distinct rows; 212 matched verbatim, the other 5 confirmed by hand (their titles were quoted with an elision or with `██` decoration) |
| Third-party claims (the Tauri/wry/rfd/dialog-plugin chain behind 11.3, 11.7) traced in the exact crate versions pinned by `src-tauri/Cargo.lock` | **pass** — each of the six links read directly in `~/.cargo/registry` |

Two limits worth stating plainly:

1. **The Ablage deadlock is a code-read, not a run.** Every link is verified in the shipped crate versions and the plugin's own docstring forbids the usage, but nobody pressed ⇧⌘E on rc.3 in this pass — GUI automation needs the Apple Events permission this machine refuses (-1743). It is a five-second manual check and it should be the first thing done with this report.
2. **A handful of agent citations attribute a real test row to the wrong file** (4.5, 5.4, 5.A among them). The rows exist and say what the verdict claims; only the filename is off. They are left as written rather than silently corrected, because a corrected citation nobody re-opened is worse than a wrong one somebody notices.

---

## How to re-run this

The agent pass is reproducible: `docs/v2/UX-AUDIT-CATALOGUE.md` is the input, the merged per-item data is reconstructible from the workflow journal, and every verdict cites a file or a command. The three things that would change the answer fastest:

1. **Fix the Ablage cluster** — make `export_board` / `import_board` `async fn` (Tauri then runs them off the main thread, and the plugin's blocking helpers become legal), and add a `print_board` command to the Tauri shell mirroring `main.swift:2468-2488`. That closes 11.7, 12.4, the 11.3 addendum, and unblocks 21.B.
2. **Add a tier-0 row per bridge command** asserting that every command `src/js/` invokes exists in *both* shells' handler lists. `print_board` would have been caught the day it diverged.
3. **Rule on 19.5 and on the 21.5 wording**, then either amend the catalogue or change the code.
